| `host/zl1-boot-image-kernel-selftest.sh` | Host-side, offline, no device. **109 checks** covering `host/zl1-boot-image-kernel.sh`. **Its fixtures are images the harness builds itself, byte by byte** — a boot header, a real gzip member, a real appended FDT, a real `IKCFG_ST`/`IKCFG_ED` blob — so a subject that reads the format wrongly cannot make a fixture agree with it, and every flag (`--opt NAME=STATE`, `--nostrings`, `--noconfig`, `--lenprefixed`, `--badconfig`, `--badmagic`, `--noappend`, `--earlyed`, `--badpage`, `--cutmember N`, `--oversize N`, `--page`, `--truncate`, `--cmdline TEXT`) plants ONE structural fact. **Every verdict is reached, including the two that are not verdicts about the kernel** — an unreadable file (exit 3, nothing claimed) and a kernel with no embedded config, whose option states must print as `NO CONFIG EMBEDDED` rather than `not set`. **Six mutations each break ONE thing in the subject** and must redden a named assertion: reading the whole blob as the Image, searching `IKCFG_ED` from zero instead of after `IKCFG_ST`, hard-coding the page size, folding "no config" into "not set" (the distinction the tool exists to keep), printing an empty difference for a pair that cannot be compared, and printing the layer table's summary with its rows dropped — an empty difference being exactly what "the two kernels were built from the same config" looks like. The last two sections run it against the REAL candidate and the REAL image this project boots and assert the stage's own claim as a LINE: their embedded configs differ in **exactly one option** while their initramfs (`sha256 ebb281ff5537d99a`) and their five appended device trees (`5b280099e84e773c`) are byte-identical. Static guards cover the ways it could write, and the fixture tree is hashed before and after a run. **docs 161 added the layer table, a sixth mutation (the summary printed with its rows dropped), and the assertions that catch the table being printed AFTER the option list — and doing that found a two-round-old defect in the FIXTURE ITSELF**: its boot header put the 16-byte `name` field where `cmdline` belongs, so the reader's `d[64:64+512].split(b'\0')[0]` started on a NUL and **every fixture in the file had read an EMPTY cmdline** — invisible until then because no assertion read a cmdline's VALUE, only that the label appeared. The new `--cmdline` fixture option and the cmdline row of the table are what exposed it, and the pair of fixtures now proves a cmdline differing only past the displayed width still reads `DIFFERS`. It carries the citation drift guard, so its count here and in `host/zl1-health-check.sh` cannot part. |
| `host/zl1-fp-kernel-probe-selftest.sh` | Host-side, offline, no device. **112 checks** covering `device/zl1-fp-kernel-probe.sh`. The fixtures are phone-shaped device trees plus a fake `/proc/config.gz`, and the discipline is the family's: a full sandbox **and** a sandbox with no `find(1)` (because the probe's search has a fallback and a fallback nothing runs is not a fallback), a `dmesg` stub carrying the core's own words (`probe of soc:qcom,qbt1000 failed with error -22` — the driver core, not the driver, prints that), and a **CANARY written into four fake device nodes** (`/dev/goodix_fp`, `/dev/qbt1000`, `/dev/qseecom`, `/dev/spidev`) asserted absent from every scenario's output, so a future revision that opens one is caught by the fixture rather than by the phone. A static guard covers the ways this could write (`> /sys/...`, `dd`, `tee`, `setprop`, `modprobe`, `mount`), with four teeth each asserted to be caught and a prose fixture asserted to be left alone. Eleven scenarios walk the ladder, including the pair that keeps **`NOT READ`** (no config was found) and **`NOT SET`** (the option is off) apart — a distinction the probe exists to make — and eight mutations each have to redden a NAMED assertion (the empty-ERE trap in the node search, the board-tree check, the `-d` guard in the no-`find` fallback, and the top-rung test both ways). It carries the citation drift guard, so its count here and in `host/zl1-health-check.sh` cannot part. |
| `host/zl1-eeprom-probe-selftest.sh` | Host-side, offline, no device. **285 checks** covering `device/zl1-eeprom-probe.sh`, the LAST row of docs 137's gap list -- and the only block on this board where **NOTHING is missing**, which is what makes its harness different from its eleven siblings'. Same transport discipline (**the stub directory IS the device**, a sandboxed `PATH` of symlinks to the real coreutils, a one-pass-per-rule token rewrite with landing counts, the token-to-fake-root invariant and named-path checks all asserted, and **one scenario per rung**), plus **two things this block forced**: a **THREE-rule rewrite** (`/proc/`, `/sys/` and **`/dev/i2c`** -- the last because the probe NAMES the userspace route to the same slave in its prose and in its verdict, and a rewrite covering only the roots it reads today would leave a future `> /dev/i2c-8` escaping to the laptop), and a **SECOND static guard, for READS**, because this probe's claim is stronger than "it writes nothing": it reads the attribute's EXISTENCE and none of its contents. The write guard has seven teeth (`printf 1 > /sys/bus/i2c/devices/8-0051/eeprom`, `dd if=/dev/zero of=` the same path, `printf 1 > /dev/i2c-8`, `modprobe at24`, a prose fixture with an arrow and a heredoc, and a `2>/dev/null` fixture), and the read guard has its own (`cat` / `od` / `wc -c <` of the attribute) plus the one case it must **NOT** flag: reading the device directory's `name` with this tree's own `rd`, which is exactly what the probe is allowed to do. **One assertion in this harness could never have matched, and it shipped that way (docs 149)**: the probe prints that `sysfs_create_bin_file() is the LAST` / `FALLIBLE thing at24_probe() does` **across two printed lines**, while the harness required the phrase on one — so the check was not a check, it was decoration, and its sibling wanted `a statement before it failed` where the verdict said `something before it failed`. The probe's own prose is the side that moved (the FM sibling one bus over prints the whole sentence on one line, which is what the requirement describes) and the two headings are now met by what the probe actually prints. **A fixture defect worth recording**: the fake root was originally named after the block, and since the probe scans the tree for the WORD (`eeprom` or `at24`) and prints how many nodes it found, a directory named `zl1-eeprom-probe-selftest` made **every** node match -- the count read 4 where the tree has 0, and the sentence "a scan for it finds 0 nodes" could never print. The directory is now named with none of the block's three words, and that is part of the fixture. Scenarios cover all twelve rungs plus: **no status at all** (the board's real shape, so the DEFAULT fixture is the board and `status-okay` is the special case -- the reverse of the FM harness, where `disabled` is the shape to catch), `disabled` for the other direction, a compatible LIST, `read-only` PRESENT (the driver tests PRESENCE, not a value), `pagesize` in all **three** states (absent / present-but-EMPTY, where `of_get_property()` returns a non-NULL pointer to nothing and the driver reads four bytes that are not there / present with MORE cells, which is truncated to the first and is the OPPOSITE of the FM block's exactly-two-cells rule), no-alias and client-under-another-bus, a driver that is registered but unbound, a bound driver with no attribute, a tree with no such node, a node with another compatible, `bare-tree` (find + nofind: an absence vs a search that could not run), and **the camera's two `qcom,eeprom@N` nodes**, which is what makes the word-scan count a reading rather than a constant. **Thirteen mutations** each redden it, and the FIRST is the shape this block invites: reading `CONFIG_EEPROM_LEGACY` -- **the option beside the right one**, for the near-miss driver that is not even in the kernel -- which blames a config line that has nothing to do with this node on a kernel that DOES build the driver. The others: the attribute rung dropped, the client rung dropped, the driver looked up under the CLIENT's name (`24c32`) instead of the driver's own (`at24`), the endianness (a DT u32 is four big-endian bytes, so host-order `od -tu4` reports 0x51 as 0x51000000 -- a plausible number and a client name that cannot exist), "could not search" collapsed into "not found", "could not read" collapsed into "not set" (**in both places it appears**, the config column AND the userspace path one section lower), an absent `status` read as off, the bus's other-device scan emptied, the word scanned for changed to `at24`, the userspace node looked up BY NUMBER, the `pagesize` read as the SECOND cell (a plausible value from the wrong cell -- the harder failure to notice), and the attribute's contents echoed into the report, which is the mutation the read guard exists for. |
| `device/zl1-nfc-probe.sh` | Runs on the device. **Read-only, and it writes nothing at all -- not even a scratch file -- and it opens no device node.** It closes the `nfc` gap from docs 137, and the block behind that row is ONE node (`/soc/i2c@75b6000/nq@28`, `qcom,nq-nci`) that carries **two generations of property names**, only one of which anything reads. Six readings make it. (1) **The node carries both spellings and they do not agree about the wiring**: `qcom,nq-ven` / `qcom,nq-irq` / `qcom,nq-firm` are TLMM pins (phandle 28), `qcom,nq-clkreq` is a PMIC pin (phandle 29) and the driver's reset line `nxp,p61-rst` is back on the TLMM, while `nxp,p61-pwr` -- which **no `.c`, `.h` or Kconfig in this tree reads** -- is a PMIC pin: so the block is two gpio controllers, and a report that said "the node has a power enable" without saying WHICH namespace it came from would be reporting a line no driver asks for as if it were the wiring. (2) **Three of the five reads are fatal and two are not**: an invalid `qcom,nq-ven` or `qcom,nq-irq`, or a missing `qcom,clk-src`, makes `nfc_parse_dt()` return -EINVAL and the probe never runs, while a missing `qcom,nq-firm` is a `dev_warn` and a missing `qcom,nq-clkreq` a `dev_err` -- so "the node is complete" and "the driver can bind" are different questions and it answers them separately. (3) **One string value is a switch**: `if (!strcmp(clk_src_name, "BBCLK2"))`, with every other value taking `goto err_free_dev`; this board carries the one name the driver supports. (4) **The config line that decides this block is outside the menu it looks like it belongs to**: `config NFC_NQ` sits in drivers/nfc/Kconfig AFTER the `endmenu` of `menu "Near Field Communication (NFC) devices"` (the menu that `depends on NFC`), so `CONFIG_NFC` does not gate it -- and the two kernels this project has in hand prove it in opposite directions: the vendor boot image's 3.18.120 kernel has `# CONFIG_NFC is not set` AND `CONFIG_NFC_NQ=y` (the driver is BUILT with the menu off) while the v63 Halium kernel this port boots has both off. **The same `CONFIG_NFC` line appears in both**, so a reader who checked the obvious line would get the same answer from a kernel that has the driver and one that does not; the probe prints both lines and decides on `CONFIG_NFC_NQ`. (5) **Two `#ifdef`s defined nowhere**: `CONFIG_NFC_HW_CHECK` and `NFC_KERNEL_BU` appear only as `#ifdef` uses, so there is no hardware presence check at probe AND NO POWER-ON either -- VEN is driven LOW and the ref clock stays off, and the chip is powered from userspace by `ioctl(NFC_SET_PWR)` (1 = on, 2 = download mode, 0 = off) on the misc device. (6) **Three nested witnesses and four names**: the i2c CLIENT exists from the device tree alone with no NFC driver built at all; the BIND needs the driver built, registered and its probe past the three fatal reads and the clock string; the MISC DEVICE (`/sys/class/misc/pn544`) needs the probe to have run to the END, because `misc_register()` comes after the clock and the gpios and every later failure path deregisters -- and of the four names, `nq-nci` is the only one that is a sysfs path, `nqx-i2c` is the id_table name, and `pn544` is also a DIFFERENT driver in this same tree. Its rungs are `tree-unscanned` / `wrong-board-tree` / `unknown-board` / `no-device-tree-node` / `no-driver-for-node` / `no-node-enabled` / `no-client` / `driver-not-built` / `driver-not-registered` / `driver-not-bound` / `no-misc-device` / `nfc-ready`, each a different next move -- and the client rung is deliberately ABOVE the driver ones, because a client the core never created cannot be fixed by any config line. **What it cannot say, and says so**: whether NFC works -- a client, a bound driver and a registered misc device are the software path in place, none of them is a card read, and the controller is still powered DOWN until something issues `NFC_SET_PWR`. It writes nothing and opens no device node (a `read()` on `/dev/pn544` waits on the IRQ and then speaks NCI to the chip). `--quiet` / `--explain` / `--help`. Exit 0 only on the top rung. See docs 146. |
| `device/zl1-fm-radio-probe.sh` | Runs on the device. **Read-only, and it writes nothing at all -- not even a scratch file -- and it opens no device node.** It closes the `fm-radio` gap from docs 137, and the block behind that row is ONE node (`/soc/i2c@75b5000/silabs4705@11`, `silabs,si4705`) that **the device tree switches off itself**. Six readings make it. (1) **`status = "disabled"` -- in all 15 LE_ZL1 trees and all three sets**, which makes this the only block in the whole project whose tree refuses its own node: every other block instrumented here carries NO `status` at all, which the device tree reads as ENABLED. A node that is not okay is never instantiated by the i2c core, so there is no client, no bind and no radio device, whatever the kernel was built with. (2) **And the obvious explanation is wrong**: `CONFIG_RADIO_SILABS=y` in BOTH kernels in hand, the vendor 3.18.120 one and the v63 Halium one this port boots -- so this is NOT the `nfc` block's shape (a config line outside the menu that gates it, off in the shipping kernel) but its MIRROR IMAGE. (3) **This option IS gated by its menu** -- `config RADIO_SILABS` sits inside `if RADIO_ADAPTERS && VIDEO_V4L2`, and `RADIO_ADAPTERS` is a menuconfig depending on `VIDEO_V4L2` and `MEDIA_RADIO_SUPPORT` -- so the probe prints FOUR config lines, because "the option is not set" has four possible parents here. That contrast is why the two probes are worth having separately. (4) **The node describes its two interrupts TWICE, through different mechanisms**: `interrupts = <0 1>` with an `interrupt-map` routing child 0 to TLMM gpio 38 and child 1 to TLMM gpio 78 -- the SAME two pins the driver reads as `silabs,int-gpio` and `silabs,status-gpio` -- and the driver acts on NEITHER, ignoring the map and calling `gpio_to_irq()` on its own gpios at OPEN time. (5) **Three gpios, two of them fatal, and two regulators, neither fatal** -- `silabs,reset-gpio` fails probe() with the GPIO'S OWN negative errno and `silabs,int-gpio` the same way, while `silabs,status-gpio` is optional (a FMDERR, and later code tests `status_gpio > 0`); the supplies are the reverse (a failed `regulator_get("va")` only defers or skips, a failed `regulator_get("vdd")` prints and continues) -- **but the voltage property beside each supply IS fatal**, because `of_property_read_u32_array(..., 2)` reads EXACTLY TWO CELLS: longer is silently truncated, shorter fails -EINVAL. The board's two supplies are one of EACH KIND, which is why both are printed: `va-supply` is `/soc/rome_vreg`, a `regulator-fixed`, i.e. a PMIC GPIO driving a fixed supply, and `vdd-supply` is an `qcom,rpm-smd-regulator` rail whose voltage the RPM owns rather than the CPU. (6) **Its write-class move is opening the device**: `silabs_fm_fops_open()` powers the chip up -- both regulators, the pinctrl active state, the three gpios -- and the first ioctl writes real commands over i2c, AND the v4l2 core puts ONE WRITABLE attribute on every radio device, `/sys/class/video4linux/radioN/debug` (`DEVICE_ATTR_RW`), a verbosity knob. **The probe writes neither and opens no device node**; the harness's write-guard teeth include both surfaces. **The identity of the radio device is its `name` file and the number is an allocation** (`RADIO_NR` is -1, so v4l2 hands out the first free number: the `name` reads `radio-silabs` while the directory is `radio0`). Four names for one block, and only the second is a sysfs i2c path: the compatible `silabs,si4705`, the driver `.name` `silabs-fm`, the `i2c_device_id` `radio-silabs`, and `radio-silabs` again as the v4l2 device name -- while `si470x`, the driver in the directory beside it, declares NO `of_match_table` at all and can never bind this node. Board-first, a nine-rung ladder (`no-device-tree-node` / `no-driver-for-node` / `no-node-enabled` / `no-client` / `driver-not-built` / `driver-not-registered` / `driver-not-bound` / `no-radio-device` / `radio-registered`), with the client rung ABOVE every driver rung because the i2c core creates the client from the tree and no config line can fix one that does not exist. Closed by `scripts/host/zl1-fm-radio-probe-selftest.sh` (266 checks) before the device runs it. |
| `device/zl1-fp-kernel-probe.sh` | Runs on the device. **Read-only in the strongest sense this project has: it opens no device node at all, not even to look, and it writes nothing at all.** It reads the fingerprint blocks **at the kernel layer** — the layer every other fingerprint document here sits above (docs 83/98/101/126 are about the store directory, the HAL and the trust store) — and the reading is per block because **this board's device tree declares two of them and the kernel in hand builds a driver for exactly one**. `/soc/spi@7579000/goodixfp@0` (`compatible = "goodix,fingerprint"`, `input-device-name = "gf318m"`, reset gpio31, irq gpio121) is the Goodix sensor the container's HAL opens `/dev/goodix_fp` for, and the config **the running kernel was built with** — read out of `/proc/config.gz`, so out of the kernel image itself rather than a defconfig on a laptop — says `# CONFIG_INPUT_GP5XX8 is not set`, so `gf_spi.c` is not in this kernel, nothing binds that node, and the HAL's `open()` cannot succeed for a reason below every layer the fingerprint documents have worked on. `/soc/qcom,qbt1000` **is** built (`CONFIG_MSM_QBT1000=y`) and creates `/dev/qbt1000` plus the input device `qbt1000_key_input` — seen on the screen in docs 70/73 — so "there is a fingerprint driver in this kernel" is TRUE and "the sensor the HAL opens has a driver" is FALSE. Verdict ladder: `tree-unscanned` < `wrong-board-tree` < `unknown-board` < `no-fingerprint-block` < `no-driver-in-source` < `driver-not-built` < `driver-not-bound` < `every-block-has-a-bound-driver`, and it names the FIRST block that failed with its own compatible and its own option. **Why it may not look closer:** qbt1000's `open()` runs an SNS QMI open and keep-alive and then an `scm_call2(TZ_BLSP_MODIFY_OWNERSHIP)` that hands the SPI BLSP block to the secure world (`release()` gives it back), and a secure-world call is the shape that has already cost this project a boot (docs 58) — so "looking at the fingerprint device" would be a state change dressed as a reading. Three traps it reads around, all measurable here: the config option (`CONFIG_INPUT_GP5XX8`), the source file (`gf_spi.c`) and the driver (`.name = goodix_fp`) are four different names, none of them the compatible; qbt1000's device-tree child (`qcom,fingerprint-sensor-ssc-spi-conn`) carries **no `compatible`** and the driver matches it by node NAME, which is why this block is invisible to every DTB-derived reading in this project; and a platform node with no `reg` takes its name by climbing to the root, so the device that exists is `soc:qcom,qbt1000`, not `/sys/bus/platform/devices/qcom,qbt1000`. And the sibling driver's trap is why it reads the BOUND state rather than the initcall: `gf_init()` registers a fixed major and then returns 0 **regardless of whether `spi_register_driver()` succeeded**. It is step **04o** of `host/zl1-post-recovery-capture.sh`'s default set, and it is the instrument that closed the last row of docs 137's gap list — the one docs 156 found by measuring what the coverage table leaves out. It **does not claim** the sensor works, that the HAL's failure is caused by the missing driver, or that anything here is a fix. |
| `device/zl1-eeprom-probe.sh` | Runs on the device. **Read-only, and it writes nothing at all -- not even a scratch file -- and it opens no device node; it also reads NO byte of the attribute it is about.** It closes the LAST `eeprom` gap from docs 137, and the block behind that row is ONE node (`/soc/i2c@75b6000/at24@51`, `compatible = "atmel,24c32"`, `reg = <0x51>`) that carries **exactly two properties and no `status`**. Six readings make it. (1) **THIS IS THE ONLY BLOCK WITH NOTHING MISSING**: `CONFIG_EEPROM_AT24=y` in BOTH kernels in hand with `CONFIG_SYSFS=y` (the Kconfig's other parent), and the tree leaves the node enabled -- so the ladder does not look for the one missing thing, it says **which of the three witnesses is present**, and the top rung is deliberately called `eeprom-exposed` and not `*-ready`. (2) **IT BINDS ONLY BECAUSE TWO TABLES AGREE, AND THE MATCH TRAVELS THROUGH A NAME THE TREE NEVER SPELLS**: `at24_of_match[]` has one entry and the i2c driver's `.name` is `at24` (the sysfs directory), while the CLIENT is the compatible with the **vendor prefix stripped** by `of_modalias_node` -- `atmel,24c32` -> `24c32`, which IS an `at24_ids[]` entry, and `if (!id->driver_data) return -ENODEV` is what a compatible that strips to nothing in that table would hit **before the chip is touched**. That entry is also where the size (4096 bytes), the 16-bit address flag and the 1-byte write cap come from, so "is it a 24c32" is the TABLE's answer, not the tree's. (3) **THE TREE'S TWO PROPERTIES AND THE DRIVER'S TWO PROPERTIES DO NOT INTERSECT**: `at24_get_ofdata()` asks for `read-only` (PRESENCE) and `pagesize`, and this tree answers neither -- so the attribute is created WRITABLE and `page_size` stays 1. The length rule here is the **opposite** of the FM block's exactly-two-cells one: `of_get_property()` returns a pointer for a property of ANY length and the driver takes the FIRST cell, so longer is truncated rather than refused, and only a present-but-EMPTY property is undefined -- the probe prints all three states. (4) **THE READ PATH AND THE WRITE PATH ARE THE SAME FILE**: `/sys/bus/i2c/devices/8-0051/eeprom` runs `i2c_transfer()` on read as well as on write, and it holds a board's calibration or serial data -- so "look at the EEPROM" and "overwrite the EEPROM" differ by one redirect, and the probe reads the attribute's existence and none of its contents. `sysfs_create_bin_file()` is the last FALLIBLE statement of `at24_probe()`, which is why the attribute's presence is the strongest of the three witnesses. (5) **THE WORD IS NOT THE BLOCK, measurably**: a scan for `eeprom` finds **0 nodes** on this board's trees (the node is `at24@51`), and what a reader's grep actually lands on is the CAMERA's two `qcom,eeprom@N` nodes; the near-miss `drivers/misc/eeprom/eeprom.c` (`.name = "eeprom"`, **no `of_match_table`**) is not even in the running kernel, since `CONFIG_EEPROM_LEGACY` is not set in either -- "cannot bind" and "is not there" are two different things. (6) **THERE IS A SECOND PATH ONE LEVEL BELOW THE DRIVER**: `CONFIG_I2C_CHARDEV=y` means `/dev/i2c-N` can address slave 0x51 with no at24 driver in the path; the probe prints whether that node exists and opens neither it nor the attribute. The ladder is `tree-unscanned` / `wrong-board-tree` / `unknown-board` / `no-device-tree-node` / `no-driver-for-node` / `no-node-enabled` / `no-client` / `driver-not-built` / `driver-not-registered` / `driver-not-bound` / `no-eeprom-attribute` / `eeprom-exposed`, and the client rung sits ABOVE every driver rung for the same reason as its two siblings. It is step **04n** of the one-boot capture chain. |
| `device/zl1-sleep-and-throttle.sh` | Runs on the device. `--status` (default) / `--quiet` / `--explain`. **Read-only, and it is the first instrument here that reads files which ARE writable on the device**: `cpuidle/state*/disable`, `thermal_zone*/mode`, `thermal_zone*/policy` and `lpm_levels/parameters/sleep_disabled` are exactly what a script asking "why is this thing hot" would want to nudge -- and the last one **IS the fix for the question this probe asks**. It touches none of them: harness-proven byte-for-byte, one static tooth per file, and `--explain` names all four. Seven sections: (1) the sleep question read **twice**, because docs 121 had to correct itself on which reading is load-bearing: the power parameters in **this boot's** `/proc/cmdline` (whole lines -- the value is the reading), and `/sys/module/lpm_levels/parameters/` read **generically, by directory**, with `sleep_disabled`'s value and its mode. The cmdline says what the boot was TOLD; the parameter file says what the driver HAS now; that file is mode **0664** -- writable while the device runs -- so the fix for this cause is a WRITE, not a new boot image and not a flash, and the two readings can disagree (a disagreement is printed as a reading: "the cmdline asked for it off and the driver has it on" is what the fix looks like from here). The verdict follows the LIVE value, and only when BOTH halves agree the ladder is allowed may the cause be called ABSENT; a half that could not be read is UNKNOWN and exits 1, but a readable half is enough to decide -- and the verdict then says which half it could not read; (2) the **live** device tree's ladder under `soc/qcom,lpm-levels`, walked recursively, each level's `label` and `qcom,latency-us` -- 'the ladder exists and is unused' and 'the ladder does not exist' are different findings; (3) the **consequence**, which is the actual evidence: every core's every cpuidle state's `name`/`usage`/`time`/`disable` with the deepest state's share as a **ratio** (the sysfs ABI does not document `time`'s unit, and a ratio cancels it -- this device reports three units for three thermal zones, docs 96, so an unlabelled counter should not be arithmetic'd on); (4) `msm_thermal/parameters/*` read **by directory, not by name**, plus the cooling devices, of which there are none registered; (5) each zone's `type`/`mode`/`policy` and **deliberately NOT its temperature** -- `zl1-thermal.sh` owns that table and a second table is a second chance to get the units wrong; (6) per-core governor/cur/min/max/online, i.e. how much of the two known heat fixes is installed; (7) a verdict naming **all three** heat causes independently, each PRESENT/ABSENT/UNKNOWN, with UNKNOWN being a fact about the instrument and making it `exit 1`. `--quiet` changes what is printed and never what is decided -- asserted by comparing the two exit codes, after a version where a `continue` in the per-core loop left the tallies at their initial values and a quiet run reported a cpuidle cause it had just read successfully as UNKNOWN. The byte-level reading behind the correction -- the `__param` entry decoded as `struct kernel_param`, and the calibration against `usbcore.autosuspend`/`usbfs_memory_mb` (0644) and `nousb`/`blinkenlights` (0444) that makes the decode a reading rather than a guess -- is in [`docs/ubuntu-touch/evidence/lpm-sleep-disabled-param-2026-09-24.txt`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/evidence/lpm-sleep-disabled-param-2026-09-24.txt) |
| `device/zl1-lpm-ladder-trial.sh` | Runs on the device. `--status` (default) / `--apply` / `--revert` / `--settle SECS` / `--keep` / `--allow-keeper` / `--explain`. **The experiment that decides the third heat cause, and the first thing in this repository whose fix is a runtime WRITE to a live power parameter**: docs 121 corrected itself to `lpm_levels.sleep_disabled` being a *module* parameter of the built-in driver (mode 0664) rather than a `__setup` boot argument, which turns "reflash boot" into "`printf 0 > /sys/module/lpm_levels/parameters/sleep_disabled`". `0664` proves the file is writable and NOT that writing to it changes what the SoC does, so this script is built to be able to **REFUTE** the hypothesis rather than demonstrate it. `--status` writes nothing. `--apply` writes exactly one file and puts it back, and **three refusals come before any write** — (A) the panic→EDL escalation must be disarmed (`download_mode == 0`, else a hang costs a finger rather than a reboot); (B) the cpuidle counters must be readable (they are the whole measurement, and UNREADABLE IS NOT ZERO — a zero there looks exactly like "the ladder came on and was never entered"); (C) no debug keeper may be running (a CPU that is never idle does not enter a deep idle state, so the reading would be about the keeper, not about the parameter — `--allow-keeper` proceeds and the verdict calls the reading CONFOUNDED). Each refusal names which one failed and leaves the device untouched. The write path is **proved before it is used** (the current value is written back to itself and read), the revert is a one-liner written to a file **on the device** (`/tmp`) so losing the session does not lose the undo, a `trap` reverts on every exit path, and there is deliberately **no background timer** — the value does not survive a reboot, so the next boot is also the undo. Exit codes: 0 a measurement (REFUTED included), 1 INCONCLUSIVE/CONFOUNDED, 2 not the zl1, 3 REFUSED, 4 the write happened and something after it failed. **Gate A reads EVERY `/sys/module/*/parameters/download_mode`, not the first match** (docs 125): the unit that arms that policy clears all of them and fails itself if any did not clear, so a gate satisfied by the first match can be satisfied by a knob that is not the one the policy clears -- and this gate is a refusal, so what that buys is exactly the finger it exists to save. The device was measured to expose exactly one such parameter (`msm_poweroff`), so the harness carries a decoy (`qcom_poweroff`, sorting after) and drives both directions. It does not print temperatures and does not measure heat — `zl1-thermal.sh --ab` owns that. Two defects found while building it, both worth carrying: the DEEPEST cpuidle state was being taken from cpu0's state NAME, which made every verdict a statement about the shallowest one; and `: > "$DELTAS"` is **fatal in dash** when the redirect fails (`:` is a special builtin), so the shell exited **2** — which this script defines as "not the zl1" — before its own check could report, while the trap still reverted correctly. It is `printf` now, which is an ordinary builtin and therefore returns a status instead of dying. |
| docs 120 — the modem line's four rungs | Offline (docs 120). The offline readings behind it, all reproducible read-only in [`docs/ubuntu-touch/evidence/modem-offline-readings-2026-09-24.txt`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/evidence/modem-offline-readings-2026-09-24.txt) with `debugfs` and two parsers: the DTB asks for `modem` with `qcom,pil-self-auth` set; `/vendor/firmware` in the Android vendor image has **no** `modem.mdt`/`modem.b*`/`mba.mbn` (only `modem_pr/`, which is RF config); the `modem` partition is **FAT16** and `MODEM.MDT` is the image's **ELF header**, whose 26 program headers match the 20 `MODEM.B*` files one for one by size — and the one absent index (14) is a header with `p_filesz = 0`, so it is not a missing piece; Android mounts that partition at `/vendor/firmware_mnt` with `shortname=lower`, and the UT rootfs has **no** `/lib/firmware` (nor `/usr/lib/firmware`, `/lib` being a symlink to `usr/lib`) and no fstab entry for the partition. **A hypothesis with a name, not a finding** — the probe measures it |
| docs 121 — the third cause of the heat | Offline (docs 121). The offline readings behind it in [`docs/ubuntu-touch/evidence/sleep-and-throttle-offline-readings-2026-09-24.txt`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/evidence/sleep-and-throttle-offline-readings-2026-09-24.txt): all **five** of the boot image's DTBs carry a complete `soc/qcom,lpm-levels` ladder — whole-SoC `system-wfi`/`system-ret`/`system-fpc` (100/350/11000 us), both clusters' L2, and per-core levels — while **every** zl1 cmdline, the stock image's own and the v63 boot image's verbatim copy of it, carries `lpm_levels.sleep_disabled=1`. And that boot kernel, decompressed and decoded properly, says what the knob IS: a **module parameter of the built-in `lpm_levels` driver** -- mode **0664** in the kernel's `__param` section, while the entry beside it, `cpuidle.off`, is 0444 -- which makes the fix for this cause a runtime WRITE rather than a new boot image and a flash. Decompressing that boot kernel has a trap worth knowing before trusting a `strings` result: the kernel section is followed by the DTBs, so `gzip` ends in `BadGzipFile(b'\xd0\r')` and the read has to be `zlib.decompressobj(31)` with the `unused_data` being the DTBs. And a DT value like `<100>` has three NUL bytes, so a naive printable-text renderer prints `"   d"` where the number is |
| docs 122 — testing the third cause | The experiment and its 124-check harness, offline (docs 122). `device/zl1-lpm-ladder-trial.sh` writes one file and puts it back, refuses unless `download_mode == 0` (so a hang reboots rather than entering EDL), the cpuidle counters are readable (UNREADABLE IS NOT ZERO — a zero there is the reading the whole experiment turns on), and no debug keeper is running (a CPU that is never idle would make the reading about the keeper). The verdict can print **REFUTED**, the write path is proved with a same-value write before anything is changed, the undo is written to a file on the device, and there is deliberately no background timer because the value does not survive a reboot. |

## Reading the GPS line (four cheap questions before touching anything)

The recorded framing — "the QMI channel to the modem fails" — was a misreading of one log line, and it
was settled by reading the HAL source rather than the log (doc 82). In `LocApiV02.cpp` the message
`Failed to get features supported from QMI_LOC_GET_SUPPORTED_FEATURE_REQ_V02` is logged inside the
`else` branch of `locClientOpen()`, so it **can only appear when the QMI client opened**, it is not
fatal, and `open()` returns FAILURE in exactly one place: the branch that logs `locClientOpen failed`.
The action that has never happened is `gnssStart` / `u_hardware_gps_start`, and the most likely reason
is that nobody has ever asked for a position — the UT daemon's own client tool cannot ask, it only
reads and writes two switches.

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `device/zl1-gps-probe.sh` | Runs on the device. Read-only unless `--test-gps`. Prints: the unit's **real** `ExecStart` and which drop-in supplied it (`systemctl cat`, the only honest check that the android-aware wrapper is in effect), the daemon's pid and whether it is in the container's PID namespace (compared by `ns/pid`), the two switches through `lomiri-location-serviced-cli` (plus its usage verbatim, so a wrong subcommand shows up as such), the log counts **split by which process could have written each string** (docs 102) — a vendor library in the container goes to logcat, the UT-side daemon and the library it loads go to that unit's journal, and a pattern that was counted in the wrong log read as a structural 0, which pointed the whole diagnosis one layer too low; the old list had two patterns **in no binary in any image** (`set_gps_service_callbacks`, `Unable to get GPS service` — the latter is the framework's `GnssLocationProvider`, which this path does not go through) and five journal-side patterns were never counted at all. The journal query is boot-scoped (`-b`), the only kind that survives this device's broken clock — the `custom.location.*` properties read **inside** the container, whether a GNSS HIDL service is registered at all (`lshal` under `nsenter -p -m`), and `/etc/gps.conf`. `--test-gps` additionally runs `/usr/bin/test_gps -c` with the TLS preload and the hybris library path: it starts one tracking session through the legacy `gps.h` HAL, which is **not** guaranteed to be the same implementation the HIDL path uses, so neither a pass nor a fail translates by itself. **And it ends with a VERDICT (docs 109)**, which it did not before: nine sections of evidence and no bottom line meant the first real capture was read as "the GPS probe produced no verdict" while those sections held the deepest evidence this chain has ever had — on 2026-09-23 they show `locClientOpen failed` = 0 with `Failed to get features supported` = 2 and `gnssSetCapabilitesCb` = 2 (both strings are in `/mnt/vendor-ro/lib64/libloc_api_v02.so`, the container's vendor GPS HAL, so a non-zero count is that process having run — and per docs 82 the features line is only written *after* `locClientOpen()` succeeded), plus `u_hardware_gps_set_position_mode` in the adapter. So the door is not what is blocking, and the earlier "the request was never made" reading is no longer supported by the recording. The verdict names the rung the evidence stops at (`no-container`, `wrong-namespace`, `trust-store-refused`, `qmi-open-failed`, `reaches-vendor-hal`, `daemon-only`, `no-gnss-listing`, `no-evidence`), is built from the SAME count variables the table prints, and it now READS the `lshal` listing instead of refusing to (docs 110): the build tree carries lshal, the built `liblshal.so` carries its literals verbatim, and `hash` -- the `R` column -- is assigned in exactly one place, `fetchBinderizedEntry()`, i.e. lshal's FIRST table only. So a row in table 1 means hwservicemanager lists the service (registered) and `R = Y` means it answered `interfaceChain()`/`getHashChain()` (live); the `Server`/`Threads` columns are read only for a table-1 row, because lshal's own description of table 2 says its `Server` column can be ignored. The listing is captured WHOLE, not `grep gnss`: the blank lines are the table separators, and dropping them is what made the first capture unreadable. By that reading the 2026-09-23 recording says the container's `android.hardware.gnss@1.0::IGnss/default` IS registered. The rungs are `no-container`, `wrong-namespace`, `trust-store-refused`, `qmi-open-failed`, `reaches-vendor-hal`, `gnss-not-registered`, `no-gnss-listing`, `daemon-only`, `no-evidence` -- and the name is printed on its own `VERDICT: <name>` line, because prose cannot be grepped. Exit codes: 0 only for `reaches-vendor-hal` — a positive finding about the layers above it, not a claim that GPS produces a fix. |
| docs 105 — the GPS door's first client | Offline (docs 105). **`device/zl1-location-request.sh --request` is not the only client this port has, and not the best one: the preinstalled Weather app is a permitted, real one.** `weather.ubports_weather_6.2.0`'s QML (compiled into `lomiri-weather-app`) contains `import QtPositioning 5.12` and `PositionSource { active: settings.detectCurrentLocation }`, and its own `.apparmor` `policy_groups` lists **`location`** first — which the baked profile `click_weather.ubports_weather_6.2.0` spells out as the system-bus DBus rules for `com.lomiri.location.Service` / `.Session`. The lock's keeper is on the device too: `lomiri-location-service-trust-stored.service` and its `-wayland` twin are both enabled and mutually exclusive by `ConditionPathExists` on `/run/user/%U/mir_socket_trusted`, so exactly one agent runs (`MirAgent` or `WaylandAgent`) — which is why "the daemon's journal" and "the trust-stored unit's status" have to be read together, a swallowed exception being indistinguishable from a denial. So the first request needs **no** bypass: launch Weather with the launcher the camera already uses (`device/zl1-camapp-launch.py`, app-agnostic), turn on "detect current location", and watch. Caveats recorded there: Weather is the first app other than the camera to be launched this way, it also links `libQt5WebEngine.so.5` (never exercised here), and its desktop `Exec` is `lomiri-weather-app-migrate.py lomiri-weather-app` — starting the binary directly skips the migration step. The `detectCurrentLocation` setting lives in the app's QML LocalStorage at `~/.local/share/weather.ubports/Databases/<md5(weather.ubports)>.ini`, the path its own migrate script computes. |
| `device/zl1-location-request.sh` | Runs on the device. `--status` (default, **read-only**) / `--request [--seconds N]` / `--enable-testing` / `--disable-testing` / `--explain` / `--quiet`. The GPS experiment doc 93 made possible. `--status` prints the chain link by link: is the unit enabled/active, its **effective** `ExecStart` and which drop-ins are in `${UNIT}.d`, the service's pid and whether it shares PID 1's namespace, whether `/usr/bin/getprop` **is the v63 stub** (and therefore that both `custom.location.*` answers are empty by construction), what those two properties actually print, whether `com.lomiri.location.Service` and `core.trust.dbus.Agent.LomiriLocationService` are owned on the system bus, whether `libubuntu_platform_hardware_api` is mapped into the daemon, and the unit's own status. `--request` then makes the request the whole port has never made: a `PositionSource { name: "lomiri" }` QML client under `/usr/lib/qt5/bin/qmlscene` with the **offscreen** platform, printing every `positionChanged`/`sourceError`/`validityChanged`/tick line and diffing the daemon's pid and restart count across it — using the already-built client library is deliberate, because it means no D-Bus signature (the `Criteria` struct) has to be reverse-engineered. `--enable-testing` installs the one-file drop-in that opens gate 1 (the image's own `TRUST_STORE_PERMISSION_MANAGER_IS_RUNNING_UNDER_TESTING` switch) — **that is a permission bypass**: while it is in place anything on the device can obtain its location, so it is the user's call, and `--disable-testing` is the undo. `--explain` prints both drop-ins and the trade-off and changes nothing. Read-only in `--status`/`--request`/`--explain`: nothing written outside `/tmp`, no property set, no restart. **The consent notice cannot be silenced** (docs 97): `--quiet` used to suppress it along with everything else, so `--quiet --enable-testing` installed the permission bypass with nothing printed on stdout or stderr — it now goes through a separate `warn()` that writes to stderr, on the rule that "be quieter" means fewer read-only findings and never "do not tell me what I am about to give up". Every mode is covered offline by `host/zl1-loc-fp-selftest.sh`, which asserts the drop-in's **actual contents** (exactly one `Environment=` line), that the other drop-in survives `--disable-testing`, and that no mode makes a systemd call that changes the device. |

The lever worth knowing about before running any of it: the daemon's wrapper has a `custom.location.fake`
branch that runs the **whole UT location stack on a fake coordinate** (`dummy::Provider` +
`custom.location.lat/lon`), with no Android GPS involved. That is what separates "the UT side is fine"
from "the HAL is broken" — at the cost of one restart of a D-Bus-activated service.

**【Corrected by [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/93-gps-the-door-is-a-client-request-and-the-two-levers-are-dead.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/93-gps-the-door-is-a-client-request-and-the-two-levers-are-dead.md): that branch can never be taken on this device, and neither can the wrapper's other lever.** Both read `getprop`, and the v63 boot hook overwrites `/usr/bin/getprop` with a `/bin/sh` stub on every boot whose `case` table has no `custom.*` arm — with no default argument both properties print nothing, and `setprop` is a no-op in the same hook. So the wrapper always runs `--provider gps::Provider --provider remote::Provider` with the trust-store testing bypass **off**, and every session request dies on `TrustStorePermissionManager` (no AppArmor profile for a shell caller, and no trust-store answer otherwise) as the opaque `Error creating session`. The working replacements are two drop-ins, neither applied: `Environment=TRUST_STORE_PERMISSION_MANAGER_IS_RUNNING_UNDER_TESTING=1` (the image's own switch, and a genuine permission bypass — the user's call) and an `ExecStart=` override that restores the fake-location A/B without a boot-image change. `device/zl1-location-request.sh --explain` prints both. Note the A/B is *still* behind that lock, so it needed the first drop-in to be observable at all — a second, independent reason it could never have produced a result. **】**

## Measuring the heat (and it is a delta, not a temperature)

An absolute temperature on this phone says nothing — the battery is charging and the ambient changes.
Doc 72 answered "what is burning" with hand-run commands, and its conclusion came from the
**difference** between two windows (SIGSTOPping the debug keeper dropped tsens1/tsens8/pm8994 by
5.5/6.1/2.7 C), so the instrument below exists to make that shape reproducible: one window, then
`--ab` for the deltas either side of changing one thing. Two rules it encodes, both learned the hard
way in doc 72: `top`'s instantaneous percentages on this kernel cannot be added up, so every CPU
number is `/proc/<pid>/stat` fields 14+15 accumulated over a window (HZ=100, integer deltas); and
**`iowait` is kept apart from user/sys**, because "2.4 cores in the kernel" turned out to be iowait
accounting.

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `host/zl1-thermal-selftest.sh` | Host-side, offline, no device. 48 checks. Three of them are about `host/zl1-health-check.sh` itself rather than the arithmetic — the page is run in a directory of its own with stderr kept separate and both must come back empty, plus a static rule that no unescaped backtick survives in its prose. Found by a stray empty file named `file` in the repository root: **backticks inside a double-quoted string are command substitution**, so seven emphasised phrases in the page's prose were being EXECUTED as commands when their line printed (`/` made `bash: /: Is a directory` and dropped the character from the printed sentence; `tr | grep | sed ||` failed to parse, so the substitution failed and the rest of that printed line vanished; `nsenter -m -- test` ran; `` `: > file` `` created the file). The static half failed on the two lines that document the defect, which is how it was confirmed to work. Drives the **real** `device/zl1-thermal.sh` (sed-rewritten into a fake root) and the **real** `host/zl1-health-check.sh` against a fake device whose thermal zones deliberately use **all three conventions at once** — which is the only way this arithmetic is testable, and is what the zl1 actually does. The numbers are the device's own: the fake `/proc/stat`, `/proc/<pid>/*`, cpufreq nodes and zone values are the 2026-09-22 snapshot, and the A/B scenario rewrites the zones and stops a fake keeper's CPU ticks mid-run, so the assertion is that **doc 72's measurement survives the instrument** (`-6.1 C (58.0 -> 51.9)`, `-5.5 C (55.8 -> 50.3)`) rather than that some number moved. It pins the three shapes the old arithmetic produced as text that must NOT appear (`0.x C` on a tsens zone, `0.x C` on `msm_therm`, a board/battery zone named hottest); the guards (an unknown type flagged `assumed`; a 1000x-mis-scaled zone flagged `IMPLAUSIBLE` **and** refused a place in the hottest pick; a snapshot where every zone is implausible reported as such instead of inventing one); the delta ordering; the union fix (a keeper that stopped is reported at all); `read_stat`'s two-line assertion (doc 81's own bug, driven by feeding a `/proc/stat` with no aggregate line); the flags; that the fake `/proc` and `/sys` are byte-identical afterwards and the temp dir is gone; and — the anti-drift check — that the instrument and the health check name the **same** hottest zone with the same value from the same six zones, which is what stops the two copies of the table separating silently. Stubs `ip`, `ping` and `ssh` in front of PATH (the `ssh` stub answers the health check's `echo ok` probe with `ok` and everything else with the canned device fields), and wraps the A/B run in `timeout` so a hang fails the test instead of hanging the harness. `--keep` leaves the fake root and the rewritten scripts; exit 0 only if all 48 pass (the row said 44 until 2026-09-24: a hand-typed count, which is the defect this tree keeps finding). See [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/96-three-thermal-units-in-one-snapshot.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/96-three-thermal-units-in-one-snapshot.md). |
| `device/zl1-thermal.sh` | Runs on the device. `--seconds N` / `--top N` / `--ab` / `--hold N` / `--quiet`. One window: how many cores are busy and in what (user/sys/irq/softirq/**iowait**), context switches/s, loadavg, **D-state thread count**, the top N processes by tick delta, every thermal zone with the hottest named, every core's governor/cur/max, and memory (`MemTotal`/`MemAvailable`/`Swap` -- `MemAvailable` is the number doc 72 never read, and `top`'s `used` is not pressure). The container's memory is looked for where it actually lives (`/sys/fs/cgroup/memory/lxc/*/`); when this kernel has no memory hierarchy at all it says so rather than printing the hierarchy's ROOT -- which is the whole system, not the container, and is the mistake doc 72 section 8 made (docs 87: a container-side `free` reports this device's MemTotal, because `CONFIG_MEMCG` is off and the rootfs ships no lxcfs). `--ab` runs a second window after a plain `sleep` (never a keypress — it runs over ssh) and prints the per-process and per-thermal-zone **differences**, which is the whole point. Process names are read from `/proc/<pid>/comm`, not from `/proc/<pid>/stat`'s parenthesised field, which may contain a space and would shift the utime/stime fields onto the wrong process. Read-only: writes only to `/tmp`. **Since 2026-09-24 this is the instrument `host/zl1-heat-fix-chain.sh` drives** (`--ab --seconds --hold`, docs 128): it is scp-ed to the device, runs its two windows around the two heat fixes, and its differences are archived as `06b-heat-ab.txt` -- so a `--ab` change here changes what the heat chain reports. Verified end to end on the host's kernel (x86, 88 cores) — the run that verified it also caught a format-string bug one `%s` short that printed a plausible-looking but completely wrong answer, now an assertion — but **not** on the zl1, whose thermal-zone/cpufreq/cgroup paths are the unverified part. See [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/81-the-heat-line-has-an-instrument-now.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/81-the-heat-line-has-an-instrument-now.md). **The zones do not share a unit, and this script did not know that until it was run against a fake device root built from the zl1's own snapshot (docs 96).** On this device `tsens_tz_sensor*` report **deci-degC** (558 = 55.8 C), `pm8994_tz`/`battery` **milli-degC**, and `msm_therm`/`quiet_therm`/`pa_therm*`/`emmc_therm` **plain degC** — three conventions in one snapshot, and nothing in the file says which is which. Dividing everything by 1000, which is what it used to do, reported the SoC at **0.6 C**, made the `hottest:` line unable to ever name a tsens zone (by raw value the hottest reading on the device is the *smallest* number in the table), and rendered doc 72's 5.5 C keeper result as **`+0.0 C`**. It now scales by a `type`-keyed table, flags a type it does not know (`assumed`) and a value outside -40..160 C (`IMPLAUSIBLE`, then excluded from the hottest pick), and prints the raw value and unit on the hottest line (`raw 580 = deci-degC`) so the arithmetic can be checked in the head. Two more defects came out of the same exercise: the per-process `--ab` walked only window B's top list, so **a process that went quiet was reported nowhere** (doc 81 section 3 described the behaviour it did not have) — now the union of both lists; and the per-zone `--ab` sorted signed deltas, putting the biggest mover last whenever the biggest mover was a drop — now sorted by the size of the change either way. |

## Measuring the audio path (the speaker)

The chain is `paplay -> pulseaudio sink.primary_output (module-droid-card, Active Port
output-speaker) -> android.hardware.audio@2.0-service in the container -> snd_device(2:
speaker-stereo) + mixer path "low-latency-playback smartpa" -> the MSM8996 `TERT_MI2S_RX` backend`,
i.e. the audio leaves the SoC over the **tertiary MI2S to an external smart amplifier**, not through
the internal WCD9335 speaker PA. Every link of that has been read back on 2026-09-22 and none of it
is broken (doc 74); what no measurement here can decide is whether the speaker is **audible** — that
needs the user's ear, and the test script is built to be run while they are holding the phone.

Two traps, both recorded because both produce a wrong answer rather than an error:

* **The host's `amixer` cannot read this card** (`amixer -c 0` -> "Mixer load sysdefault:0 error: No
  such device"), while `/system/bin/tinymix` runs straight from the host (Halium symlinks
  `/system -> /android/system`) and prints all 2392 controls. Reaching for `amixer` here concludes
  "no codec". And unlike the sensors HAL, reading the codec needs **no** `nsenter` — do not carry
  that rule over.
* **A 440 Hz test tone is not a fair audibility test** on a small phone speaker; the first attempt
  used one at amplitude 12000/32767 and silence there would prove nothing. Use ~2 kHz at high
  amplitude.

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `device/zl1-audio-test.sh` | Runs on the device. `--seconds N` / `--hz F` / `--amp A` / `--sink NAME` / `--status`. Plays a generated tone through the normal PulseAudio path and reads the codec **during** playback and again after it, so the two columns show whether the HAL actually configured the amplifier (playing: `MultiMedia5` On, `Speaker Volume 5`, `Digital Gain 56`, `Boost 9V`, channel enables On; idle: Off / 1 / 40 / 6.5V / Off). `--status` is read-only. See [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/74-the-speaker-path-is-complete-in-software.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/74-the-speaker-path-is-complete-in-software.md). |

## Finding out *where* the device crashed

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `hybris-crash-hunt.sh` | Runs a libhybris helper on the device (any `/usr/bin/test_*`, or a full command path such as `/usr/share/ubuntu-touch-session/lsc-wrapper`), captures the kernel's core dump, rebuilds a sysroot out of the core's own `NT_FILE` list, and prints the faulting address, the nearest symbol, the faulting instruction and the frame-pointer chain. `HYBRIS_TEST_PRELOAD` sets the run's `LD_PRELOAD`, `HYBRIS_TEST_ARGS` appends arguments. This is how the Phase 5 display failure was traced to `__ctype_get_mb_cur_max+8` inside Android `libc.so` — see [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/40-the-display-died-below-lomiri.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/40-the-display-died-below-lomiri.md). Needs `gdb-multiarch` on the host; needs nothing on the device (no compiler, no rootfs change — `core_pattern` is `/proc`, and cores land on `/userdata`). |
| `hybris-crash-hunt.sh --from-pid PID` | The same analysis for a process that **hangs** instead of crashing: `SIGABRT` it, which makes the kernel write the core, and everything after that is identical. `RLIMIT_CORE` belongs to the target process, so the script calls `prlimit --pid … --core=unlimited` first — without it no core is written and the newest `core.*` is the *previous* session's, which analyses perfectly and means nothing. It also verifies the core's name is `core.<comm>.<pid>`, and reads `/proc/PID/exe` before the kill because `comm` is truncated to 15 characters. This is how the Phase 5 hang was traced to `waitForHwServiceManager` — see [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/42-the-wait-that-could-never-finish.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/42-the-wait-that-could-never-finish.md). |

## Why the device was in EDL (a kernel panic goes there by default)

Doc 80 §7 recorded the 2026-09-23 trip into Qualcomm EDL and never attributed it. Reading the kernel
tree that built the flashed image turned up a path that needs nothing from anyone: **a kernel panic**.
`download_mode` is a compiled-in **1**, and `CONFIG_MSM_FORCE_WDOG_BITE_ON_PANIC=y` forces the reset
that follows it, so the SoC comes back up with the dload flag set and the bootloader enters EDL
instead of booting. `reboot edl` does the same thing deliberately; the PMIC undervoltage path
(`dload_on_uvlo`) is off. There is **no software exit**: EDL runs the SoC's boot ROM, so the kernel
that would run any "leave EDL" code is precisely what is not running — a long power press, or
nothing. Two witnesses survive the reset, and they are what this reads: **pstore/ramoops** (enabled,
and wired in the DT: 1 MiB at `0x91500000` with `android,ramoops-dump-oops`), and the kmsg archive
the drain script keeps (`keep/boot-<boot_id>/`, which after a UT → EDL → UT round trip holds the boot
that died). See [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/86-edl-has-a-cause-a-panic-and-the-evidence-survives.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/86-edl-has-a-cause-a-panic-and-the-evidence-survives.md).

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `device/zl1-edl-postmortem.sh` | Runs on the device, and it is the **first thing after the device comes back from EDL**. Read-only. It asks the device itself (`/proc/config.gz`, not the build tree) whether the panic → EDL path is armed and what `download_mode` is, then reads both witnesses — `/sys/fs/pstore/*` and the last snapshot of the `keep/boot-*/` archive that the drain's own `archive.log` names — against a set of death signatures (`Kernel panic`, `Unable to handle kernel`, `BUG:`, `Going down for restart`, `PC is at`, `WDOG: watchdog bite`, …) and gives a three-branch verdict. An **empty pstore proves nothing** (ramoops has to survive the reset for the file to exist at all, and that has never been verified on this device), so "no witness" is reported as "no witness" with exit 1, never as "no panic". `--quiet` / `--full`; exit 0 = ran, 1 = a witness could not be read, 2 = not the zl1. Its branches were exercised against a synthetic tree; **that run caught two real bugs**, the worse being a backtick inside a double-quoted `say` that was command substitution, so the script **executed `reboot edl`** — harmless on the host, and on the phone it would have put the device straight into EDL. **Its synthetic exercise is now a committed harness** — `host/zl1-edl-postmortem-selftest.sh`, seven device states, 34 checks — and re-running it caught a **third** bug the hand-run missed: the fallback snapshot lookup was `ls -tr "$K"/boot-*/` **without `-d`**, which lists the archive directories' *contents*, so the loop received bare file names and the next `ls` ran in the caller's working directory (the demonstration in the evidence log has it matching an unrelated host `.log`). That path exists to find the driver's own t≈2 s line — the one thing this script calls the most valuable it can find — and the failure was silent, because "not in that snapshot" is a normal output line. Same bug family as the `ls -t` bug above: that fix corrected one site and left its sibling (docs 95). **And on the first device run (2026-09-25) it printed a FALSE ATTRIBUTION, which docs 161 dated to the pattern set**: the list held bare `watchdog` and bare `Call trace`, and **every healthy boot prints both** — `msm_watchdog … MSM Watchdog Initialized` and the six backtraces that follow its six boot-time `WARNING:`s. Measured on `keep/boot-0488bd3c-…/boot-84s.log`, a boot that provably did not die because it produced five later snapshots: `WARNING:` x6, `Call trace:` x6, `watchdog` x2, and every strong pattern 0. So it printed `*** contains a death signature ***` and `FOUND: a kernel oops/panic is on record` for a boot that was merely WARNINGing, and that verdict was nearly written down as the cause of the EDL trip. Two changes: the ambiguous pair moved **out of the verdict** into a **counted reading** (`not-as-a-signature: 'Call trace' x6, 'watchdog' x2`), because `Call trace` x6 is a fact about a log while "an oops is on record" is a claim this script may only make when it can **name the line**; and every match now names its pattern (`matched 'Kernel panic'`), because an anonymous verdict cannot be audited — which is exactly how this survived both a hand-written harness and a device run. **The second witness then had the same defect one layer down (docs 161): WHICH archive it read was decided by `ls -dt`, and this device's clock is wrong, so all four archives share an mtime and that order is arbitrary — measured, it ranked the boot that actually died LAST. `no death signature` reads identically either way. The pointer is now the newest `keep/archive.log` entry (`prev=<boot_id>`) **whose archive still exists**, the `ls -dt` path survives only as a fallback that is **labelled as clock-dependent**, and the report says which rule answered (`chosen by: …`). |
| `host/zl1-edl-postmortem-selftest.sh` | Host-side, offline, no device. Runs the **real** `device/zl1-edl-postmortem.sh` against **nine** synthetic device states — the script is sed-rewritten so its device paths point into a fake root, and only a `sh -n` plus a rewrite check stand between the harness and a silently untested copy. It checks three things per state, not one: the verdict text (**and what it must not say** — state F has no pstore and no kmsg directory at all, and must report "no witness" with exit 1 while never turning that into "no panic"), the exit code (0 = ran, 1 = a witness was unreadable, 2 = not the zl1), and that **nothing was executed and nothing was written**: 23 stubs (`reboot`, `qdl`, `fh_loader`, `fastboot`, `mount`, `dd`, `systemctl`, `i2cset`, …) are prepended to PATH and record their invocation into a marker file instead of acting, and a `find -printf '%p %s %T@'` snapshot of the fake root is diffed before and after. That guard is aimed at this script in particular: its first version executed `reboot edl` while merely printing a report. Seven states cover FOUND from pstore, FOUND from the kmsg archive, the driver's "Failed to set secure DLOAD mode" line through the live snapshot **and** through the `keep/` fallback, armed-and-no-witness, `download_mode=0`, both directories missing, and not-the-zl1. **An eighth state (docs 161) is the one the other seven could not stand in for: a HEALTHY boot in the shape a real boot log actually has** — six `WARNING:`s each followed by its `Call trace:`, plus the watchdog driver's own two init lines, with the counts taken from `keep/boot-0488bd3c-…/boot-84s.log` rather than invented. The states above that represent "nothing happened" hold **one fabricated line**, so none of them could ever reach the detector, and **a negative fixture that cannot trigger the detector proves nothing about the detector** — which is why the false positive above survived this harness. Against the pre-fix revision (`git show <rev>:scripts/device/zl1-edl-postmortem.sh`) state H alone reddens **four** checks, one of which is `the verdict DOES say [contains a death signature]`. It also asserts that a FOUND **names its pattern** in both witnesses. **A ninth state (docs 161) is the same lesson one layer up: WHICH archive is the boot that died.** The pointer was `ls -dt "$K"/boot-*/`, and this device's clock is wrong, so every archive directory carries the same mtime and that order is **arbitrary** — measured on 2026-09-25, where it ranked `boot-0488bd3c` first and put the boot that **actually died**, `boot-92165447`, **last**. Reading the wrong archive prints `no death signature` in exactly the same words. The rule is now the drain's own `keep/archive.log` (the newest `prev=` entry whose archive still exists), `ls -dt` survives only as a labelled fallback, and the verdict **says which rule answered** — because "the newest archive" is a claim about the clock until proven otherwise. State I makes the two rules **disagree** (two archives, the clock ranking the wrong one first, the log naming the right one), and includes the case that broke the first version of the rule: a `prev=` line naming a boot whose archive the newest-4 rule had already pruned. It also **asserts the fixture itself disagrees** — the first version created both directories inside the same millisecond and `ls -dt` broke the tie arbitrarily, so the state passed while being unable to tell the two rules apart (`'ls -dt' ranked 'boot-dead00', not boot-old01, so this state cannot tell the two rules apart`). Against the pre-fix revision, state I alone reddens **four** checks, including `the verdict DOES say [boot-old01 is a *previous*]` — it named the WRONG boot as the candidate. `--keep` leaves the fake root and the stub bin for inspection; **57 checks**, exit 0 only if all pass. See [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/95-the-first-commands-after-recovery-are-offline-verified.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/95-the-first-commands-after-recovery-are-offline-verified.md). |

## Auditing the two Android images offline (no device, and it answers real questions)

The container's Android side is two images this port already has on the host: the **vendor partition**
(`vendor.img`) and the **Android system** the container mounts (`system.img`, and the identical
`android-system-zl1-halium-candidate.img` at `/data/system.img`). Almost everything about why a vendor
process fails to start is decided inside those two files, which means it can be answered while the
phone is in EDL, unplugged, or in a drawer. The trap is that "the library is there" is not a link
test — a `DT_NEEDED` is a **class-free soname**, so the question is always "*is there a library of
this ELF class, in a directory this class searches?*". That is what this script asks, for every
executable and every library at once, and then it separates what matters from what does not: an
*executable* with an unresolved dependency is a failure init will repeat forever, while a *library*
nothing references is just a file left behind. See
[`../docs/ubuntu-touch/90-the-one-process-that-cannot-link-and-it-is-32-bit.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/90-the-one-process-that-cannot-link-and-it-is-32-bit.md).

Two facts about the backups themselves, both found the hard way on 2026-09-23 and both worth knowing
before reaching for one:

* **Use `2026-06-07-adb-root-staged`, never `2026-06-07-adb-root-exact`.** The `-exact` copy passes
  its own `SHA256SUMS` (31/31 OK) and its filesystems are still unopenable (`mount(2): Structure
  needs cleaning`, and `debugfs` sees garbage in the inode table). A checksum proves the bytes were
  not corrupted in transit; it does not prove the bytes are a filesystem. The script refuses the
  `-exact` path with a warning, and its default is the staged one.
* `system.img` and the `-candidate.img` written to `/data/system.img` are the **same image** (same
  filesystem UUID), so auditing one audits the container's `/system`.

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `host/zl1-vendor-link-audit.sh` | Read-only, host-side, no device. Mounts the two images with `loop,ro,noload` (no journal replay, nothing written), then for every ELF under `bin/` and `lib*/` resolves each `DT_NEEDED` against the same-class sonames in `/vendor/lib{64}` + `/system/lib{64}`, and reports two lists. **Executables**: each broken one is cross-referenced against the init `.rc` that names it, so the report distinguishes "init will restart this forever" from "nothing starts it, it is a dead file" — the whole point, since the images carry both. **Libraries**: each broken one is cross-referenced against its referencers, printed with *their* ELF class, because a 64-bit referencer needs the 64-bit sibling and does not make the 32-bit file a fault. Exit 0 = no init-started executable is broken, 1 = at least one is. `--exec-only` / `--quiet` / `--vendor DIR --system DIR` for already-mounted trees / `--images DIR`. Its findings on the stock images: exactly one started process cannot link (`/vendor/bin/vsimd`, 32-bit, needs the 32-bit `libQSEEComAPI.so`, which exists nowhere — see doc 90), and two binaries nothing starts (`mdm_helper`, `mdm_helper_proxy`, missing `libmdmimgload.so`). Its **first version was flaky** — the same image reported different unrelated executables as broken from run to run, because membership was tested with `printf | grep -qx` under `set -o pipefail`, whose exit status is not a reliable boolean; the `case`-pattern version is stable across runs. A finding list that changes between identical runs would have invented port faults. |

## The bionic TLS-slot shim

On aarch64 glibc the thread pointer's slot 1 (`TP+8`) is `tcbhead_t::private`, which glibc never
reads or writes — and that is where bionic keeps `TLS_SLOT_THREAD_ID`. Nothing in this libhybris
fills it, so `__get_thread()` is NULL and any `__get_bionic_tls()` read crashes. These two build
and install a superset of `libtls-padding.so` that fills the slot. See
[`../docs/ubuntu-touch/41-bionic-tls-slot-is-never-filled.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/41-bionic-tls-slot-is-never-filled.md).

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `tlsfix/tlsfix.c` | The shim: the original 128-byte TLS padding, a constructor that points `TP+8` at a zeroed fake `pthread_internal_t`, **and a `pthread_create` interposer** that fills the slot on every thread created later — the constructor only reaches the thread it runs on. Freestanding (`-nostdlib`); the only libc entry points are `malloc`/`free`/`dlsym`, and both failures fall back to the real `pthread_create`. |
| `tlsfix/build-tlsfix.sh` | Cross-builds it with `clang --target=aarch64-linux-gnu` + `lld`, then checks the result really has a `PT_TLS` segment, an `DT_INIT_ARRAY` entry, the `tls_padding` symbol, an exported `pthread_create`, and **no version definition** (a versioned `pthread_create` would not bind glibc's `pthread_create@GLIBC_2.34` references and the interposer would silently never run). |
| `tlsfix/install-tlsfix.sh` | `--mount` / `--unmount` / `--status`. `--mount` `scp`s the build to `/userdata/zl1-tlsfix/shadow/` and bind-mounts it over `/usr/lib/aarch64-linux-gnu/libtls-padding.so`, which is the one file `lsc-wrapper` preloads — lightdm builds the compositor's environment itself, so an `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` on `lightdm.service` never reaches it, but replacing that file does. Runtime only: gone after a reboot, and `--unmount` undoes it. |

    Three details in `hybris-crash-hunt.sh` are worth knowing before trusting a trace
that came out of it. First, the offset it reports for a frame is the ELF's
link-time vaddr, obtained by taking the address's file offset from `NT_FILE` and
mapping it through the module's own program headers — **not** `address − mapping
start`, which is only the same thing when the mapping covering the start of the
file has `p_vaddr == 0` (`libc.so` yes, `libhidltransport.so` no: 0xa000). Second,
for a run, `SIGABRT` on a process whose `RLIMIT_CORE` is 0 produces no core at all,
and the script then finds the newest one lying around. Third, and for the same
reason: the script picks the core by **time** (`touch` a stamp, then `find -newer`),
never by name — the kernel truncates `%e` to 15 characters, so
`/usr/bin/lomiri-location-serviced` writes `core.lomiri-location.<pid>`, and a
name-based match silently falls back to a stale core from a previous boot. On
2026-09-21 that stale core analysed perfectly and reproduced doc 45's Mir fault in
a different process; see [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/49-two-cores-that-are-not-tls-wifi-stuck-at-wcnss-and-a-trip-into-edl.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/49-two-cores-that-are-not-tls-wifi-stuck-at-wcnss-and-a-trip-into-edl.md).

## Starting a UT app from the host (and the EGL underneath it)

An app is not `test_camera`: it is a Qt process whose EGL goes through the glvnd dispatcher
(`libEGL.so.1.1.0` → `/usr/share/glvnd/egl_vendor.d/10_libhybris.json` first, `50_mesa.json` second),
whereas the shell and `test_camera` either bypass glvnd (Mir's own platform plugins dlopen
`libEGL_libhybris.so.0` directly) or are raw Wayland clients. There is **no wayland QPA plugin** on
this rootfs, so `ubuntumirclient` is the only platform plugin a Qt app here can use. Three things
have to be right at once — the container's PID namespace, **the running session's own environment**
(read from `/proc/<shell pid>/environ`; a hand-built environment reproduces the EGL assertion), and
running **as the session's uid** (`ZL1_AS_UID=32011`; the session bus rejects any other peer) — and
getting them wrong looks like a platform fault. See
[`../docs/ubuntu-touch/80-the-ut-camera-app-starts-and-our-preload-was-breaking-egl.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/80-the-ut-camera-app-starts-and-our-preload-was-breaking-egl.md).

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `device/zl1-camapp-launch.py` | Runs on the device. Launches one app with the session's environment: it reads `/proc/<shell pid>/environ` (NUL-separated, which the device's dash cannot do), drops the shell's `MIR_SERVER_*`/`QT_QPA_PLATFORM`, sets `MIR_SOCKET` + `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=ubuntumirclient`, `chdir`s into the app's package (the app finds its own QML from the current directory), then `setgid`/`setuid`s to `ZL1_AS_UID` as the **last** step (root has to read the environment and the runtime dir first). `ZL1_SET_<NAME>=<value>` overrides one session variable for an experiment, `ZL1_PRELOAD_EXTRA` appends to `LD_PRELOAD`, and arguments after the shell pid are passed to the target — which is how the EGL probe below runs. |
| `device/zl1-egl-probe.py` | Runs on the device (through the launcher). Reimplements qtmir's EGL initialisation in ctypes, with no Qt and no app in the picture: what `libEGL_libhybris.so.0` actually exports, who glvnd gives `EGL_DEFAULT_DISPLAY` to, what `mir_connect_sync` + `mir_connection_get_egl_native_display` return, and whether `eglInitialize` succeeds. This is the tool that separated "the platform is broken" from "this app is broken" — and then found the difference: the same binary and environment initialise hybris' EGL fine **without** `libcfi-shadow-init.so` preloaded and fail with it. |
| `cfi-shadow/cfi-shadow-init.c` | Fixed 2026-09-23. It resolved the real `android_dlopen` with `dlsym(RTLD_NEXT, "android_dlopen")` only. `RTLD_NEXT` searches the objects after this one in the **global** scope, and `libhybris-common.so.1` also arrives inside the **local** scope of a library glvnd dlopens (`libEGL_libhybris.so.0`), which `RTLD_NEXT` cannot see — so the shim answered **NULL to every hybris dlopen in the process** and cached that failure permanently. `test_camera` never saw it (it has `libcamera.so.1` as a `DT_NEEDED`, so the global scope was populated before the first call); every Qt app did, as hybris' EGL stopping half-way and glvnd falling through to Mesa, which needs `/dev/dri` and cannot work here. `resolve_real()` now falls back to `dlopen("libhybris-common.so.1", RTLD_NOW\|RTLD_GLOBAL)`, logs which route was needed, and `prime()` returning 0 leaves the state un-primed so the next call retries. Build with `cfi-shadow/build.sh`; the previous binary is kept on the device as `libcfi-shadow-init.so.orig-20260923`. |

## The Android-side libraries the stock image is missing

`libui_compat_layer.so`, `libhwc2_compat_layer.so` and `libhidltransport.so` are
Android-side objects that the host graphics stack reaches through libhybris, and
the stock LeEco image either does not have them or has one that cannot work
here. These scripts fetch the link-time inputs, build the objects and put them
in front of the compositor. See
[`../docs/ubuntu-touch/42-the-wait-that-could-never-finish.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/42-the-wait-that-could-never-finish.md)
and
[`../docs/ubuntu-touch/43-binder-does-not-cross-a-pid-namespace.md`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/43-binder-does-not-cross-a-pid-namespace.md).

| Script | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `hybris-shims/fetch-android-libs.sh` | Copies the device's `/android/system/lib64` libraries out to `out/stubs/`. Read-only against the device. They are link-time inputs, so the shim's ABI is the device's ABI rather than a guess. |
| `hybris-shims/build-hybris-shims.sh` | Builds `libui_compat_layer.so` from the Halium tree with the AOSP prebuilt clang (standalone: it names the header paths Soong would have supplied), and produces the patched `libhidltransport.so`. Both steps verify the result's shape — soname, exported symbols, and that the patch changed at most four bytes and the file length not at all. Deterministic for a given `ld.lld`. |
| `hybris-shims/build-hwc2-compat-layer.sh` | Builds `libhwc2_compat_layer.so` with the Android build system, because that one is a HIDL `composer@2.1` client and needs hidl-gen's output. Checks the three host prerequisites that each cost a confusing failure — ImageMagick (`bootanimation`'s `$(error)` fires during product config), a `python` → python2.7 shim (two Soong genrules are still Python 2), and `ALLOW_MISSING_DEPENDENCIES=true` (unrelated lineage modules abort kati). Prints the symbols the rootfs's `libhwc2.so.1` looks up that this build does not export; it does not NULL-check those, so a missing one is a jump to address 0. |
| `hybris-shims/build-platform-api-libs.sh` | Builds `libubuntu_application_api.so` and `libbiometry_fp_api.so` — the Android-side halves of the libhybris bridge for GPS and fingerprint, which this port had never built. Both `Android.mk` were already in the build graph (`out/.module_paths/Android.mk.list`), so this is one `m` request, not a patch; nothing in `device/leeco/zl1/` ever named either module in `PRODUCT_PACKAGES`, which is why ninja never built them. Same three host prerequisites as `build-hwc2-compat-layer.sh`, plus two traps it adds: `envsetup.sh` must be sourced with `set +u` (it indexes one past an array on purpose and bash ≥ 4.4 aborts the `source`), and the result must be taken from `system/lib64` — the product out also holds an arm copy under the same name, which builds fine and fails only at load. Prints which GPS backend went in (`BOARD_HAS_LEGACY_GPS_HAL` chooses silently), which decides whether the library talks to the HIDL gnss service or the old `hardware/gnss.h` shim. See [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/55-*`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/55-the-bridge-libraries-built-and-hwbinder-does-not-cross-pid-namespaces-either.md). |
| `hybris-shims/install-platform-api-libs.sh` | `--install` / `--remove` / `--status`. Stages the two libraries in `/userdata/zl1-hybris/lib/` and gives `lomiri-location-service` and `biometryd` a **second** drop-in carrying `HYBRIS_LD_LIBRARY_PATH` — second so that it and `install-system-tls-preload.sh` cannot clobber each other, since systemd merges drop-ins. These are system units and do not go through `lsc-wrapper`, which is why the compositor could see that directory and they could not. Took both from `failed (Result: signal)` to `active` and closed the `pc=0x0` class. `--status` reports four separate things — library present, variable set, unit active, and whether the running process actually mapped the library — because only the last one distinguishes "the symbol resolved" from "the service is happy". See [`../docs/ubuntu-touch/55-*`](../docs/ubuntu-touch/55-the-bridge-libraries-built-and-hwbinder-does-not-cross-pid-namespaces-either.md). |
| `hybris-shims/install-container-ns-services.sh` | `--install [unit…]` / `--remove` / `--status`. Runs `lomiri-location-service`, `biometryd`, `sensorfwd` and `bluebinder` inside the container's PID namespace, because Android binder — both `/dev/binder` and `/dev/hwbinder` — only completes a transaction between two processes in the same one. Doc 43 found that for binder; doc 55 measured it for hwbinder (`lshal` from the host: 0 registered services; from the container: 134), doc 60 measured it a third time for `sensorfwd` — same binary, same 25 s, `Could not find remote object for sensor service.
